Decreases

Removing Bromine gas from vessel decrease the concentration of Br2 gas
in the system.
Thus the system reestablishes dynamic equilibrium by partially counteracting
this imposed change by favouring the forward reaction - as that reaction
produces bromine gas.
As more NOBr2 is being consumed to increase yield of Br2, the yield of
NOBr2 decreases and thus concentration of NOBr2 decreases. ^FGOuPFnH

a reaction is only reversible if reactants can be
consumed to form products and products can be 
consumed to form reactants. this is only possible if
the forward and reverse reaction both can occur,
thus activation energy of forward and reverse reaction
..?

in the diagram on the left, the activation energy of the
reverse reaction is much greater than the activation
energy of the forward reaction as Ea of reverse = Ea
of forward + Enthalpy of system, thus it is non-reversible

in the diagram on the right, activation energy is low such
both forward and reverse reaction can occur thus it is
reversible. ^BGrF9ybz

a reaction is reversible if the reactants are forming products and the
products are forming reactants. This only occurs if both the reverse AND forward
reaction occur in a system and that is only possible if the Ea for both
forward and reverse reaction are relatively low.
Diagram on left; activation energy of forward reaction is low, however
activation energy for reverse reaction is high - thus reaction is not reversible.
However, diagram on right : Ea for both reverse and forward reaction is
relatively low so this reaction is reversible. ^J5TqCTj8

Decreases

removing bromine gas from the system decreases partial pressure
of Br2.

to restablish dynamic equuilibrium, the system partially counteracts
the imposed change by favouring the forward reaction : as that
produces bromine gas whilst reverse doesnt.

thus, equilibrium shifts to the left, and as NOBr2 is consumed
in the forward reaction, the paritla pressure of NOBr2 decreases
and thus concentration decreases. ^SUOAHEHA

A reaction is defined as reversible if products
are producing reactants and reactants are
also producing products. this can only occur
if the activation energy of the forward
and reverse reaction is not significantly different
from each other (?)

in the diagram, in the left reaction, the activation
energy of the reverse reaction is significantly
more than the activation energy of the forward
reaction. thus this reaction is more likely to be
non-reversible. On the right, the activation energy
of the reverse reaction isn't significantly higher
than the activation energy of the forward
reaction, thus this reaction is more
likely to be reversible ^f5YkUhgt

a reaction is only reversible
if the reactants are forming
products and products
are forming reactants. this only occurs if both
reverse and forward reaction
occur in a system and this
is only possible if the Ea
for both the forward
and rvs reaction are relatively
low.
as seen above, in the diagram
on the left, the Ea for forward reaction
is low, however activation energy
for reverse is high - thus reaction is NOT
reversible. However, on the
diagram on the right - Ea for both
reverse and forward is low
so reaction is reversible ^PXmrDqmV

Decrease

If you remove bromine gas from the vessel, µore bromine gas has to
fill up the volume and thus partial pressure is decreased (icky wording)

the system restablishes dyanmic equilibrium by partially counteracting
the decrease in partial pressure. it does this by favouring the forward
reaction, as the forward reaction produces bromine gas.

thus, equilibrium shifts to the right, and as NOBr2 is being consumed,
the concentration of NOBr2 also decreases ^XeBS637T

Decrease

By removing SO3, you are decreasing the partial pressure of SO3.

Thus, system establishes dynamic equilibrium by counteracting the 
decrease in partial pressure of SO3. It does this by favouring the
reverse reaction, as that produces SO3 and thus increases
the amount(?) of SO3 in the system and thus the partial pressure
of SO3.

Equilibrium shifts to the left, and as CS2 is being consumed in the reverse
reaction, the partial pressure of CS2 (g) decreases ^Ef2Y6yKa

Kc = 0 ^2SQqhs3J

[CO2]/1 ^dflM3HxA

By decreasing the volume of the vessel, as temperature is kept constant
pressure will increase and thus partial pressure of all gases in the
system increases.

LCP states that the system restablishes dynamic equilibrium by counteracting
the increase in partial pressure of all gases. Thus, the system favours
the reverse reaction, as that consumes more moles of gas (5:3).

Thus, equilibrium shifts to the left. Partial pressure of CS2 would
increase upon volume decreasing, then decrease partially until
equilibrium is restablished. ^HPUNTCzT

1 ^Zqmjf1Tj

[H+]^2[S2O32-] ^Tg2TvW8a

CO2 ^oYKW7yza

Decreases partial pressure of carbon disulphide gas.

As sulphur trioxide from reaction mixture is removed, partial
pressure of sulphur trioxide decreases.

Thus, system reestablishes equilibrium by counteracting
the decrease in partial pressure of sulphur trioxide - thus
the it favours the reverse reaction as it produces
SO3 thus increasing partial pressure of SO3.

Thus, equilibrium shifts to the left, and
SO3 is produced thus partial pressure of SO3 increase.

As CS2 is consumed, partial pressure of CS2 decreases. ^6vp5ckeD

If the volume of vessel is decreased at constant
temperature, pressure of all gases increase and
thus partial pressure of all gases also increase.

by LCP, system establishes equilibrium by counteracting
the increase in partial pressure. It does this by favouring
the reverse reaction, which consumes more moles of gases
(5 moles) than it produces (3 moles).

Thus, equilibrium shifts to the left, as CS2 is
consuemd in the reverse reaction, partial pressure
of CS2 decreases.
SO3 is produced in the reverse reaction, thus 
partial pressure of SO3 increases ^t8OsOuhr

			"y": -1294.338956422683,

			"width": 750.8793334960938,
			"height": 225,

			"groupIds": [],

			"updated": 1708992663528,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "Decreases\n\nRemoving Bromine gas from vessel decrease the concentration of Br2 gas\nin the system.\nThus the system reestablishes dynamic equilibrium by partially counteracting\nthis imposed change by favouring the forward reaction - as that reaction\nproduces bromine gas.\nAs more NOBr2 is being consumed to increase yield of Br2, the yield of\nNOBr2 decreases and thus concentration of NOBr2 decreases.",
			"rawText": "Decreases\n\nRemoving Bromine gas from vessel decrease the concentration of Br2 gas\nin the system.\nThus the system reestablishes dynamic equilibrium by partially counteracting\nthis imposed change by favouring the forward reaction - as that reaction\nproduces bromine gas.\nAs more NOBr2 is being consumed to increase yield of Br2, the yield of\nNOBr2 decreases and thus concentration of NOBr2 decreases.",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "Decreases\n\nRemoving Bromine gas from vessel decrease the concentration of Br2 gas\nin the system.\nThus the system reestablishes dynamic equilibrium by partially counteracting\nthis imposed change by favouring the forward reaction - as that reaction\nproduces bromine gas.\nAs more NOBr2 is being consumed to increase yield of Br2, the yield of\nNOBr2 decreases and thus concentration of NOBr2 decreases.",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 218
		},

		{
			"type": "text",
			"version": 776,
			"versionNonce": 1468513856,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"id": "BGrF9ybz",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"angle": 0,
			"x": 1685.9267128041722,
			"y": -514.0087884548457,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"width": 575.4195556640625,
			"height": 375,
			"seed": 173775220,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"boundElements": [],
			"updated": 1708992663529,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "a reaction is only reversible if reactants can be\nconsumed to form products and products can be \nconsumed to form reactants. this is only possible if\nthe forward and reverse reaction both can occur,\nthus activation energy of forward and reverse reaction\n..?\n\nin the diagram on the left, the activation energy of the\nreverse reaction is much greater than the activation\nenergy of the forward reaction as Ea of reverse = Ea\nof forward + Enthalpy of system, thus it is non-reversible\n\nin the diagram on the right, activation energy is low such\nboth forward and reverse reaction can occur thus it is\nreversible.",
			"rawText": "a reaction is only reversible if reactants can be\nconsumed to form products and products can be \nconsumed to form reactants. this is only possible if\nthe forward and reverse reaction both can occur,\nthus activation energy of forward and reverse reaction\n..?\n\nin the diagram on the left, the activation energy of the\nreverse reaction is much greater than the activation\nenergy of the forward reaction as Ea of reverse = Ea\nof forward + Enthalpy of system, thus it is non-reversible\n\nin the diagram on the right, activation energy is low such\nboth forward and reverse reaction can occur thus it is\nreversible.",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "a reaction is only reversible if reactants can be\nconsumed to form products and products can be \nconsumed to form reactants. this is only possible if\nthe forward and reverse reaction both can occur,\nthus activation energy of forward and reverse reaction\n..?\n\nin the diagram on the left, the activation energy of the\nreverse reaction is much greater than the activation\nenergy of the forward reaction as Ea of reverse = Ea\nof forward + Enthalpy of system, thus it is non-reversible\n\nin the diagram on the right, activation energy is low such\nboth forward and reverse reaction can occur thus it is\nreversible.",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 368
		},
		{
			"type": "text",
			"version": 615,
			"versionNonce": 344606144,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"id": "J5TqCTj8",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"angle": 0,
			"x": 1623.3332538248035,
			"y": -41.32973881108683,
			"strokeColor": "#e03131",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"width": 822.4193115234375,
			"height": 200,
			"seed": 276070988,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"boundElements": [],
			"updated": 1708992663529,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "a reaction is reversible if the reactants are forming products and the\nproducts are forming reactants. This only occurs if both the reverse AND forward\nreaction occur in a system and that is only possible if the Ea for both\nforward and reverse reaction are relatively low.\nDiagram on left; activation energy of forward reaction is low, however\nactivation energy for reverse reaction is high - thus reaction is not reversible.\nHowever, diagram on right : Ea for both reverse and forward reaction is\nrelatively low so this reaction is reversible.",
			"rawText": "a reaction is reversible if the reactants are forming products and the\nproducts are forming reactants. This only occurs if both the reverse AND forward\nreaction occur in a system and that is only possible if the Ea for both\nforward and reverse reaction are relatively low.\nDiagram on left; activation energy of forward reaction is low, however\nactivation energy for reverse reaction is high - thus reaction is not reversible.\nHowever, diagram on right : Ea for both reverse and forward reaction is\nrelatively low so this reaction is reversible.",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "a reaction is reversible if the reactants are forming products and the\nproducts are forming reactants. This only occurs if both the reverse AND forward\nreaction occur in a system and that is only possible if the Ea for both\nforward and reverse reaction are relatively low.\nDiagram on left; activation energy of forward reaction is low, however\nactivation energy for reverse reaction is high - thus reaction is not reversible.\nHowever, diagram on right : Ea for both reverse and forward reaction is\nrelatively low so this reaction is reversible.",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 193
		},
		{
			"type": "text",
			"version": 507,
			"versionNonce": 74839616,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"id": "SUOAHEHA",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"angle": 0,
			"x": 1604.8222024489155,
			"y": 273.31498527511354,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"width": 666.4193725585938,
			"height": 300,
			"seed": 625789132,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"boundElements": [],
			"updated": 1708992663529,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "Decreases\n\nremoving bromine gas from the system decreases partial pressure\nof Br2.\n\nto restablish dynamic equuilibrium, the system partially counteracts\nthe imposed change by favouring the forward reaction : as that\nproduces bromine gas whilst reverse doesnt.\n\nthus, equilibrium shifts to the left, and as NOBr2 is consumed\nin the forward reaction, the paritla pressure of NOBr2 decreases\nand thus concentration decreases.",
			"rawText": "Decreases\n\nremoving bromine gas from the system decreases partial pressure\nof Br2.\n\nto restablish dynamic equuilibrium, the system partially counteracts\nthe imposed change by favouring the forward reaction : as that\nproduces bromine gas whilst reverse doesnt.\n\nthus, equilibrium shifts to the left, and as NOBr2 is consumed\nin the forward reaction, the paritla pressure of NOBr2 decreases\nand thus concentration decreases.",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "Decreases\n\nremoving bromine gas from the system decreases partial pressure\nof Br2.\n\nto restablish dynamic equuilibrium, the system partially counteracts\nthe imposed change by favouring the forward reaction : as that\nproduces bromine gas whilst reverse doesnt.\n\nthus, equilibrium shifts to the left, and as NOBr2 is consumed\nin the forward reaction, the paritla pressure of NOBr2 decreases\nand thus concentration decreases.",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 293
		},

			"versionNonce": 1325927872,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1708992663530,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "A reaction is defined as reversible if products\nare producing reactants and reactants are\nalso producing products. this can only occur\nif the activation energy of the forward\nand reverse reaction is not significantly different\nfrom each other (?)\n\nin the diagram, in the left reaction, the activation\nenergy of the reverse reaction is significantly\nmore than the activation energy of the forward\nreaction. thus this reaction is more likely to be\nnon-reversible. On the right, the activation energy\nof the reverse reaction isn't significantly higher\nthan the activation energy of the forward\nreaction, thus this reaction is more\nlikely to be reversible",
			"rawText": "A reaction is defined as reversible if products\nare producing reactants and reactants are\nalso producing products. this can only occur\nif the activation energy of the forward\nand reverse reaction is not significantly different\nfrom each other (?)\n\nin the diagram, in the left reaction, the activation\nenergy of the reverse reaction is significantly\nmore than the activation energy of the forward\nreaction. thus this reaction is more likely to be\nnon-reversible. On the right, the activation energy\nof the reverse reaction isn't significantly higher\nthan the activation energy of the forward\nreaction, thus this reaction is more\nlikely to be reversible",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 393,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "A reaction is defined as reversible if products\nare producing reactants and reactants are\nalso producing products. this can only occur\nif the activation energy of the forward\nand reverse reaction is not significantly different\nfrom each other (?)\n\nin the diagram, in the left reaction, the activation\nenergy of the reverse reaction is significantly\nmore than the activation energy of the forward\nreaction. thus this reaction is more likely to be\nnon-reversible. On the right, the activation energy\nof the reverse reaction isn't significantly higher\nthan the activation energy of the forward\nreaction, thus this reaction is more\nlikely to be reversible",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},
		{
			"id": "PXmrDqmV",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 4189.866595555683,
			"y": -3448.0182882267304,
			"width": 463.1996154785156,
			"height": 450,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "#ffffff",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 1573250624,
			"version": 582,
			"versionNonce": 261664320,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1708992663530,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "a reaction is only reversible\nif the reactants are forming\nproducts and products\nare forming reactants. this only occurs if both\nreverse and forward reaction\noccur in a system and this\nis only possible if the Ea\nfor both the forward\nand rvs reaction are relatively\nlow.\nas seen above, in the diagram\non the left, the Ea for forward reaction\nis low, however activation energy\nfor reverse is high - thus reaction is NOT\nreversible. However, on the\ndiagram on the right - Ea for both\nreverse and forward is low\nso reaction is reversible",
			"rawText": "a reaction is only reversible\nif the reactants are forming\nproducts and products\nare forming reactants. this only occurs if both\nreverse and forward reaction\noccur in a system and this\nis only possible if the Ea\nfor both the forward\nand rvs reaction are relatively\nlow.\nas seen above, in the diagram\non the left, the Ea for forward reaction\nis low, however activation energy\nfor reverse is high - thus reaction is NOT\nreversible. However, on the\ndiagram on the right - Ea for both\nreverse and forward is low\nso reaction is reversible",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 443,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "a reaction is only reversible\nif the reactants are forming\nproducts and products\nare forming reactants. this only occurs if both\nreverse and forward reaction\noccur in a system and this\nis only possible if the Ea\nfor both the forward\nand rvs reaction are relatively\nlow.\nas seen above, in the diagram\non the left, the Ea for forward reaction\nis low, however activation energy\nfor reverse is high - thus reaction is NOT\nreversible. However, on the\ndiagram on the right - Ea for both\nreverse and forward is low\nso reaction is reversible",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},
		{
			"id": "XeBS637T",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 5145.604035961566,
			"y": -3487.7354256100352,
			"width": 707.6793212890625,
			"height": 275,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "#ffffff",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 1112684096,
			"version": 644,
			"versionNonce": 1447056832,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1708992663530,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "Decrease\n\nIf you remove bromine gas from the vessel, µore bromine gas has to\nfill up the volume and thus partial pressure is decreased (icky wording)\n\nthe system restablishes dyanmic equilibrium by partially counteracting\nthe decrease in partial pressure. it does this by favouring the forward\nreaction, as the forward reaction produces bromine gas.\n\nthus, equilibrium shifts to the right, and as NOBr2 is being consumed,\nthe concentration of NOBr2 also decreases",
			"rawText": "Decrease\n\nIf you remove bromine gas from the vessel, µore bromine gas has to\nfill up the volume and thus partial pressure is decreased (icky wording)\n\nthe system restablishes dyanmic equilibrium by partially counteracting\nthe decrease in partial pressure. it does this by favouring the forward\nreaction, as the forward reaction produces bromine gas.\n\nthus, equilibrium shifts to the right, and as NOBr2 is being consumed,\nthe concentration of NOBr2 also decreases",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 268,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "Decrease\n\nIf you remove bromine gas from the vessel, µore bromine gas has to\nfill up the volume and thus partial pressure is decreased (icky wording)\n\nthe system restablishes dyanmic equilibrium by partially counteracting\nthe decrease in partial pressure. it does this by favouring the forward\nreaction, as the forward reaction produces bromine gas.\n\nthus, equilibrium shifts to the right, and as NOBr2 is being consumed,\nthe concentration of NOBr2 also decreases",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},
		{
			"id": "Ef2Y6yKa",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 5118.318705697112,
			"y": -3032.3135763408895,
			"width": 743.2593383789062,
			"height": 300,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "#ffffff",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 305206848,
			"version": 598,
			"versionNonce": 1827202624,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1708992663530,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "Decrease\n\nBy removing SO3, you are decreasing the partial pressure of SO3.\n\nThus, system establishes dynamic equilibrium by counteracting the \ndecrease in partial pressure of SO3. It does this by favouring the\nreverse reaction, as that produces SO3 and thus increases\nthe amount(?) of SO3 in the system and thus the partial pressure\nof SO3.\n\nEquilibrium shifts to the left, and as CS2 is being consumed in the reverse\nreaction, the partial pressure of CS2 (g) decreases",
			"rawText": "Decrease\n\nBy removing SO3, you are decreasing the partial pressure of SO3.\n\nThus, system establishes dynamic equilibrium by counteracting the \ndecrease in partial pressure of SO3. It does this by favouring the\nreverse reaction, as that produces SO3 and thus increases\nthe amount(?) of SO3 in the system and thus the partial pressure\nof SO3.\n\nEquilibrium shifts to the left, and as CS2 is being consumed in the reverse\nreaction, the partial pressure of CS2 (g) decreases",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 293,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "Decrease\n\nBy removing SO3, you are decreasing the partial pressure of SO3.\n\nThus, system establishes dynamic equilibrium by counteracting the \ndecrease in partial pressure of SO3. It does this by favouring the\nreverse reaction, as that produces SO3 and thus increases\nthe amount(?) of SO3 in the system and thus the partial pressure\nof SO3.\n\nEquilibrium shifts to the left, and as CS2 is being consumed in the reverse\nreaction, the partial pressure of CS2 (g) decreases",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},

		{
			"id": "HPUNTCzT",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 5128.293987729278,
			"y": -2343.6362164621723,
			"width": 773.5393676757812,
			"height": 275,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "#ffffff",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 624065984,
			"version": 613,
			"versionNonce": 1135578688,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1708992663530,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "By decreasing the volume of the vessel, as temperature is kept constant\npressure will increase and thus partial pressure of all gases in the\nsystem increases.\n\nLCP states that the system restablishes dynamic equilibrium by counteracting\nthe increase in partial pressure of all gases. Thus, the system favours\nthe reverse reaction, as that consumes more moles of gas (5:3).\n\nThus, equilibrium shifts to the left. Partial pressure of CS2 would\nincrease upon volume decreasing, then decrease partially until\nequilibrium is restablished.",
			"rawText": "By decreasing the volume of the vessel, as temperature is kept constant\npressure will increase and thus partial pressure of all gases in the\nsystem increases.\n\nLCP states that the system restablishes dynamic equilibrium by counteracting\nthe increase in partial pressure of all gases. Thus, the system favours\nthe reverse reaction, as that consumes more moles of gas (5:3).\n\nThus, equilibrium shifts to the left. Partial pressure of CS2 would\nincrease upon volume decreasing, then decrease partially until\nequilibrium is restablished.",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 268,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "By decreasing the volume of the vessel, as temperature is kept constant\npressure will increase and thus partial pressure of all gases in the\nsystem increases.\n\nLCP states that the system restablishes dynamic equilibrium by counteracting\nthe increase in partial pressure of all gases. Thus, the system favours\nthe reverse reaction, as that consumes more moles of gas (5:3).\n\nThus, equilibrium shifts to the left. Partial pressure of CS2 would\nincrease upon volume decreasing, then decrease partially until\nequilibrium is restablished.",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},

		{
			"id": "6vp5ckeD",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 5360.990806949722,
			"y": -1691.2429759649447,
			"width": 598.5394287109375,
			"height": 350,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "#ffffff",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 1047678528,
			"version": 770,
			"versionNonce": 931921344,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1708996554539,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "Decreases partial pressure of carbon disulphide gas.\n\nAs sulphur trioxide from reaction mixture is removed, partial\npressure of sulphur trioxide decreases.\n\nThus, system reestablishes equilibrium by counteracting\nthe decrease in partial pressure of sulphur trioxide - thus\nthe it favours the reverse reaction as it produces\nSO3 thus increasing partial pressure of SO3.\n\nThus, equilibrium shifts to the left, and\nSO3 is produced thus partial pressure of SO3 increase.\n\nAs CS2 is consumed, partial pressure of CS2 decreases.",
			"rawText": "Decreases partial pressure of carbon disulphide gas.\n\nAs sulphur trioxide from reaction mixture is removed, partial\npressure of sulphur trioxide decreases.\n\nThus, system reestablishes equilibrium by counteracting\nthe decrease in partial pressure of sulphur trioxide - thus\nthe it favours the reverse reaction as it produces\nSO3 thus increasing partial pressure of SO3.\n\nThus, equilibrium shifts to the left, and\nSO3 is produced thus partial pressure of SO3 increase.\n\nAs CS2 is consumed, partial pressure of CS2 decreases.",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 343,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "Decreases partial pressure of carbon disulphide gas.\n\nAs sulphur trioxide from reaction mixture is removed, partial\npressure of sulphur trioxide decreases.\n\nThus, system reestablishes equilibrium by counteracting\nthe decrease in partial pressure of sulphur trioxide - thus\nthe it favours the reverse reaction as it produces\nSO3 thus increasing partial pressure of SO3.\n\nThus, equilibrium shifts to the left, and\nSO3 is produced thus partial pressure of SO3 increase.\n\nAs CS2 is consumed, partial pressure of CS2 decreases.",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},
		{
			"id": "t8OsOuhr",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 4755.9494037980285,
			"y": -422.74102744568114,
			"width": 578.5794677734375,
			"height": 350,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "#ffffff",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 2072346176,
			"version": 730,
			"versionNonce": 1256437312,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1708997026941,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "If the volume of vessel is decreased at constant\ntemperature, pressure of all gases increase and\nthus partial pressure of all gases also increase.\n\nby LCP, system establishes equilibrium by counteracting\nthe increase in partial pressure. It does this by favouring\nthe reverse reaction, which consumes more moles of gases\n(5 moles) than it produces (3 moles).\n\nThus, equilibrium shifts to the left, as CS2 is\nconsuemd in the reverse reaction, partial pressure\nof CS2 decreases.\nSO3 is produced in the reverse reaction, thus \npartial pressure of SO3 increases",
			"rawText": "If the volume of vessel is decreased at constant\ntemperature, pressure of all gases increase and\nthus partial pressure of all gases also increase.\n\nby LCP, system establishes equilibrium by counteracting\nthe increase in partial pressure. It does this by favouring\nthe reverse reaction, which consumes more moles of gases\n(5 moles) than it produces (3 moles).\n\nThus, equilibrium shifts to the left, as CS2 is\nconsuemd in the reverse reaction, partial pressure\nof CS2 decreases.\nSO3 is produced in the reverse reaction, thus \npartial pressure of SO3 increases",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 343,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "If the volume of vessel is decreased at constant\ntemperature, pressure of all gases increase and\nthus partial pressure of all gases also increase.\n\nby LCP, system establishes equilibrium by counteracting\nthe increase in partial pressure. It does this by favouring\nthe reverse reaction, which consumes more moles of gases\n(5 moles) than it produces (3 moles).\n\nThus, equilibrium shifts to the left, as CS2 is\nconsuemd in the reverse reaction, partial pressure\nof CS2 decreases.\nSO3 is produced in the reverse reaction, thus \npartial pressure of SO3 increases",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},
